style="height:0.7278em;vertical-align:-0.0833em;"></span><span class="mord">±</span><span class="mord">2</span></span></span></span> are vertical asymptotes: not critical numbers, but they still cut the line.</p><p><strong>Step 3: Build the first sign map (increase / decrease)</strong></p><p>Place the asymptotes and the critical number on a number line between the walls <span class="katex"><span |      ‖      (∞)
         -2      0      2</pre><p class="note">‖ = not in the domain (vertical asymptote). The sign can still flip there, so it's a cut point.</p><p>Pick one easy <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math <p class="sk-key"><span class="k-up">green</span> = rising (f′ &gt; 0), <span class="k-down">red</span> = falling (f′ &lt; 0), <span class="k-ext">●</span> max/min, <span class="k-ip">○</span> inflection point (f″ changes sign), <span class="k-asym">dashed</span> = asymptote. The strips under the graph are your sign maps: ∪ concave up, ∩ concave down.</p>
